Representability theorems via metric techniques
Kabeer Manali Rahul

Abstract
. We prove new Brown representability theorems for triangulated categories using metric techniques as introduced in the work of Neeman. In the setting of algebraic geometry, this gives us new representability theorems for homological and cohomological functors on the bounded derived category of coherent sheaves. To prove this result, we introduce the notion of a 𝒢 \mathcal{G} -approximable triangulated category, which generalises the notion of an approximable triangulated category.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
18G80(primary), 14A30

1.
Introduction

The importance and the ubiquity of triangulated categories in modern mathematics cannot be overstated. Neeman has recently initiated the use of metrics, and approximations via metrics, to study triangulated categories. These have been used to prove many important results, including three conjectures in algebraic geometry, see
[
Nee21a
,
Nee24
]
. The notion of a metric allows us to utilize analytic ideas, such as that of completions of metric spaces, to study triangulated categories.

The idea of a metric, which goes back to
[
Law73
,
Law02
]
, is that it assigns lengths to morphisms, and the length function satisfies the triangle inequality. In the world of triangulated categories, the metrics we are interested in are translation invariant, that is, the length of a morphism is the same as the length of the unique map from the zero object to the cone of the morphism, see
[
Nee20
, Heuristic 9]
.

Even though the theory is still in its infancy, it has already been used by Neeman and others to prove many remarkable results, see for example
[
Nee21a
,
Nee18a
,
Nee24
,
CHNS24
,
Nee21b
,
BCMR+24
,
DDLMR24a
,
DDLMR24b
]
.

In this work, we add to this growing theory in the following ways:

(1)

We introduce the notion of a (weakly)
\mathcal{G}
-approximable triangulated category, which is a generalisation of a (weakly) approximable triangulated category. Further, we show that most of the basic results on approximable triangulated categories have corresponding
\mathcal{G}
-approximable analogues.

(2)

Using the notion of
\mathcal{G}
-approximability, we prove new representability results. As an application, we get new representability theorems in algebraic geometry.

We go over each of these aspects in more detail in the following subsections.

1.1.
\mathcal{G}
-approximable triangulated categories

Neeman formally introduced the notion of (weakly) approximable triangulated categories in
[
Nee21b
]
, although the idea of approximability already appears in
[
Nee21a
]
. We state the definition first, and then discuss a simple example which should help in understanding the definition. The definition itself involves some technical notation, which we do not define precisely in the introduction, see
Notation

2.2
.

Definition
.

[
Nee21b
, Definition 0.21]

A triangulated category
\mathsf{T}
is said to be
weakly approximable
if there exists a compact generator
G
, a positive integer
A
, and a t-structure
(\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0},\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0})
on
\mathsf{T}
such that,

(1)

\Sigma^{A}G\in\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
and
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\Sigma^{-i}G,\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0})=0
for all
i\geq A
.

(2)

For all
F\in\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
, there exists a triangle
E\to F\to D\to\Sigma E
with
E\in\overline{\langle G\rangle}^{[-A,A]}
and
D\in\mathsf{T}^{\leq-1}
.

We say that
\mathsf{T}
is
approximable
, if further,

(2)
′

For all
F\in\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
, there exists a triangle
E\to F\to D\to\Sigma E
with
E\in\overline{\langle G\rangle}^{[-A,A]}_{A}
and
D\in\mathsf{T}^{\leq-1}
.

Here
\overline{\langle G\rangle}^{[-A,A]}
denotes the full subcategory of
\mathsf{T}
generated from the object
G
using coproducts, summands, extensions, and at most
A
suspensions and desuspensions. Further,
\overline{\langle G\rangle}^{[-A,A]}_{A}
denotes the full subcategory of
\mathsf{T}
generated from the object
G
using coproducts, summands, at most
A
extensions, and at most
A
suspensions and desuspensions. The example of the derived category of a ring below should give some intuition on these subcategories, see
Example

A
.

Important examples of approximable triangulated categories to keep in mind are the derived category of a ring, and the derived category of quasicoherent sheaves over a noetherian, separated scheme, see
[
Nee21b
, Example 3.5]
.

The first condition in the definition of approximability is a technical assumption on the compatibility of the compact generator and the t-structure, but it is the other condition which is the heart of this definition. The idea is that we can “approximate” any objects in the aisle of the t-structure
\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
by “simple objects”, that is, objects built in “finitely many steps” from the compact generator, up to an error term which is “smaller” than the object we started with. To understand this condition better, we look at the example of the derived category of a ring.

Example A
.

Let
R
be a ring. We denote by
\mathbf{D}(R)
the derived category of the abelian category of (right)
R
-modules. It has a compact generator
R
, and the standard t-structure
(\mathbf{D}(R)^{\leq 0},\mathbf{D}(R)^{\geq 0})
. We now discuss the second condition of the definition of approximability in this particular case. Consider a complex
F
in
\mathbf{D}(R)^{\leq 0}
, that is, its positive cohomologies vanish. Then, up to quasi-isomorphism, we can write the complex as,

\cdots\to P^{-n-2}\to P^{-n-1}\to P^{-n}\to\cdots\to P^{-1}\to P^{0}\to 0\to\cdots

with
P^{-i}
projective modules for all
i\geq 0
. We get the following commutative diagram for any
n\geq 0
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{-n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{-n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{-n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{-n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{-n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{-n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

This gives us maps of complexes,
E_{n}\to F\to D_{n}
, with
E_{n}
a bounded complex of projectives, and
D_{n}
with vanishing cohomology for all degrees greater than or equal to
-n
. As the maps form a degree-wise split exact sequence,
E_{n}\to F\to D_{n}
extends to a distinguished triangle,
E_{n}\to F\to D_{n}\to\Sigma E_{n}
. The complexes
D_{n}
represent “error terms” of sorts. They get smaller and smaller, in the sense that
D_{n+1}\in\mathbf{D}(R)^{\leq-n-2}\subseteq\mathbf{D}(R)^{\leq-n-1}
.

What remains to show is that the complexes
E_{n}
are built from the compact generator
R
in finitely many steps. The object
E_{n}
is a complex of projective modules in degrees
-n
to
0
. Let
P
be any projective module. Then, it is a summand of a free module, that is, it is built from
R
by direct sums and summands. Now, for any complex
E_{n+1}
of projective modules in degrees
-n-1
to
0
, we have the following commutative diagram,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P^{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P^{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P^{-n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P^{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P^{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P^{-n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

Again, this is degree-wise split, so it extends to a triangle
E_{n}\to E_{n+1}\to\Sigma^{n+1}P^{-n-1}\to\Sigma E_{n}
, where
E_{n}
is a complex of projective modules in degrees
-n
to
0
. So, inductively we get that
E_{n}
can be constructed from
R
using direct sums, summands and at most
n
shifts and
n
extensions. This is what we mean by saying that an object is “built in finitely many steps from the compact generator
R
”. Note that the argument above for
n=1
gives us the second condition of approximability for
\mathbf{D}(R)
.

Idea A
.

We see that the notion of approximability requires the following :

(1)

A nice set of compacts which generates, which is given by the compact generator
G
.

(2)

A notion of “smallness”, that is, the notion of a metric, which will allow us to make sense of the “error term” being smaller than the object we started with. We get this from the aisle of the t-structure in the case of approximability.

We generalise this by

(1)

Replacing a single compact generator, by a
generating sequence

\mathcal{G}
, see
Definition

3.8

(2)

Replacing the t-structure metric, by the more general notion of an
orthogonal metric
, see
Definition

3.1
.

This helps us define the notion of (weak)
\mathcal{G}
-approximability, see
Definition

4.1
.

Remark
.

We will in fact define a notion slightly more general than that of
\mathcal{G}
-approximability, namely that of
\mathcal{G}
-quasiapproximability, see
Definition

4.2
. It turns out that the notion of
\mathcal{G}
-quasiapproximability suffices to prove the representability theorems we want, but for the sake of simplicity, we will only discuss
\mathcal{G}
-approximability in the introduction. Note that the practical reason for considering
\mathcal{G}
-quasiapproximability is that there are examples of
\mathcal{G}
-quasiapproximable triangulated categories, which the author has not been able to prove are
\mathcal{G}
-approximable, see
Theorem

7.24
.

To further understand this notion, we now state the definition of a
(weakly) co-approximable
triangulated category. Co-approximability is a special case of
\mathcal{G}
-approximability, which utilises a co-t-structure to define the metric instead of t-structures as in the notion of approximability.

Definition A
.

[
Definition

7.8
]
A triangulated category
\mathsf{T}
is said to be
weakly co-approximable
if there exists a compact generator
G
, a positive integer
A
, and a co-t-structure
(\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0},\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0})
on
T
such that,

(1)

\Sigma^{-A}G\in\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
and
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\Sigma^{i}G,\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0})=0
for all
i\geq A
.

(2)

For all
F\in\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
, there exists a triangle
E\to F\to D\to\Sigma E
with
E\in\overline{\langle G\rangle}^{[-A,A]}
and
D\in\mathsf{T}^{\geq 1}
.

We say that
\mathsf{T}
is
co-approximable
, if further,

(2)
′

For all
F\in\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
, there exists a triangle
E\to F\to D\to\Sigma E
with
E\in\overline{\langle G\rangle}^{[-A,A]}_{A}
and
D\in\mathsf{T}^{\geq 1}
.

Remark
.

One of the motivations to define
\mathcal{G}
-approximability is that certain naturally occurring triangulated categories are not (weakly) approximable. One such important example is the homotopy category of injectives
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
for a noetherian scheme
X
, which we can easily show cannot be weakly approximable if
X
is not regular. This category agrees with the category of
ind-coherent sheaves

\mathbf{IndCoh}(X)
, see
[
Kra05
, Proposition A.1]
. It turns out that it is more natural to work with co-t-structures in this situation.

The following result shows that
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
is (weakly) co-approximable for a large class of schemes.

Theorem A
.

Let
X
be a noetherian finite-dimensional scheme. Then,

•

If
X
is a J-2 scheme, then
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
is weakly co-approximable, see
[
MR25
, Theorem 4.13]
. In fact, it is enough to assume that the regular loci of all integral closed subschemes of
X
are open.

•

\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
is co-approximable and
\mathbf{K}_{m}(\operatorname{Proj}\text{-}X)
is co-quasiapproximable for any of the following cases,

(1)

X
is regular.

(2)

X
is separated and quasiexcellent.

see
Theorem

7.19
,
Theorem

7.24
, and
Theorem

7.26
.

Similar result holds for the mock homotopy category of projectives, see
Theorem

7.24
and
Theorem

7.26
. Further, noncommutative and stacky versions of the result also hold, see
Remark

7.21
,
Theorem

7.26
, and
Theorem

7.27
.

1.2.
Representability results

One of the most important applications of the theory of approximable triangulated categories are the representability theorems proved in
[
Nee21b
]
. We first give an idea of what we mean by representability. Let
\mathsf{T}
be a
R
-linear triangulated category for a commutative ring
R
. Let
\mathsf{S}
be a triangulated subcategory. A cohomological functor
H:\mathsf{S}^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Mod}(R)
is
representable
if there exists an object
t\in\mathsf{T}
such that
H(-)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(-,t)|_{\mathsf{S}}
. There is a long history of representability theorems, that is, results which prove that
H
is representable under conditions on
\mathsf{T}
,
\mathsf{S}
, and
H
. We recall a few of them for the readers convenience, although this is by no means an exhaustive list. One of the first results of this flavour was by Brown, in the context of algebraic topology, see
[
Bro62
, Theorem I]
. This was vastly generalised to the context of (co)product preserving functors on compactly generated triangulated categories by Neeman, see
[
Nee96
, Theorem 3.1]
. This was even further generalised to the context of well generated triangulated categories, see
[
Nee01
, Theorem 1.17]
. Note that these result requires the existence of arbitrary coproducts in the triangulated category. When the triangulated category does not have all coproducts, typically more stringent conditions are required on the category. Two important results in this situation are the theorem by Bondal and Van den Bergh, see
[
BvdB03
, Theorem 1.3]
, and the improvement on their result by Rouquier, see
[
Rou08
, Theorem 4.16]
. Both of these results require that the category
\mathsf{S}
be strongly generated.

With this background, we now come to the result by Neeman in
[
Nee21b
]
, and the result proven in this work.
To state them, we first need to discuss the notion of the “closure of compacts”.

Idea B
.

As we have discussed, approximability (and
\mathcal{G}
-approximability) involves a
metric
on a category, which is used to make sense of “approximations”. Given the notion of a metric, it makes sense to talk about the
closure
of the compact objects with respect to a metric. The closure of the compacts will be denoted by
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
when in the general context, borrowing the notation for the closure from point set topology. The notation will be
\mathsf{T}^{-}_{c}
in the context of approximability (and in general when the metric arises from a t-structure), and
\mathsf{T}^{+}_{c}
when the metric comes from a co-t-structure. In all the cases we are interested in, there is a notion of bounded objects, and the full subcategory of all bounded objects in the closure of the compacts will be denoted by
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}
.

In the example of the derived category of a ring we discussed before, these give rise to familiar categories. For
\mathsf{T}=\mathbf{D}(R)
for a (right) noetherian ring
R
,
\mathsf{T}^{-}_{c}=\mathbf{D}^{-}(\textbf{mod}(R))
and
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}=\mathbf{D}^{b}(\textbf{mod}(R))
where
\mathbf{mod}(R)
denotes the abelian category of finitely generated (right)
R
-modules, see
[
Nee21b
, Example 3.1]
.
In the context of algebraic geometry, the following example is also useful to keep in mind. For
\mathsf{T}=\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(X)
for a noetherian scheme
X
,
\mathsf{T}^{-}_{c}=\mathbf{D}^{-}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
and
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}=\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
, see
[
Nee21b
, Example 3.4]
.

With the notion of the “closure of compacts”, we are now in position to explain the main representability result of
[
Nee21b
]
. For the rest of this section
R
denotes a commutative ring. The following is the result in question, after omitting some of the technicalities for the ease of understanding.

Theorem
.

[
Nee21b
, Theorem 0.3]

For a compactly generated
R
-linear triangulated category
\mathsf{T}
, we define the restricted Yoneda functor
\mathcal{Y}:\mathsf{T}\to\operatorname{Hom}([\mathsf{T}^{c}]^{\operatorname{op}},\operatorname{Mod}(R))
by sending an object
F
to the functor
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(-,F)|_{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
. Then, if
\mathsf{T}
is a “nice” approximable triangulated category, we have that,

•

The restriction of
\mathcal{Y}
to the closure of the compacts
\mathsf{T}^{-}_{c}
is full, and we have an explicit description of its essential image.

•

The restriction of
\mathcal{Y}
to the bounded objects in the closure of the compacts
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}
is fully faithful, and we have an explicit description of its essential image.

Note that as the explicit description of the essential image is known, if we have a cohomological functor on
\mathsf{T}^{c}
satisfying those conditions, we know that it is representable.

In this work, we prove the obvious generalisation of this theorem to the setting of
\mathcal{G}
-approximable triangulated categories, see
Theorem

6.22
and
Theorem

6.24
. We state the results below, again omitting some of the technicalities.

Theorem B
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a “nice”
\mathcal{G}
-approximable
R
-linear triangulated category. Then,

•

The restriction of
\mathcal{Y}
to the closure of the compacts
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
is full, and we have an explicit description of its essential image.

•

The restriction of
\mathcal{Y}
to the bounded objects in the closure of the compacts
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}
is fully faithful, and we have an explicit description of its essential image.

Note that the closure of the compacts here is computed with respect to the metric in the definition of
\mathcal{G}
-approximability.

As concrete applications of this abstract result, we have the following two results.

Theorem C

(
Theorem

8.12
)
.

Let
X
be a quasiexcellent finite-dimensional scheme which is proper over a noetherian ring
R
. Let
\mathcal{Y}:\mathbf{D}^{+}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\to\operatorname{Hom}([\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)]^{\operatorname{op}},\operatorname{Mod}(R))
be the restricted Yoneda functor.

•

The restricted Yoneda functor
\mathcal{Y}
is full on
\mathbf{D}^{+}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
, and the essential image is all the cohomological functors
H:\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Mod}(R)
such that for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
,
H(\Sigma^{n}F)
is a finitely generated
R
-module for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and vanishes for
n>>0
.

•

The functor
\mathcal{Y}
gives an equivalence between
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\cap\mathbf{K}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
and all the cohomological functors
H:\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Mod}(R)
such that for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
,
H(\Sigma^{n}F)
is a finitely generated
R
-module for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and vanishes for
|n|>>0
.

Noncommutative and stacky analogues to
Theorem

C
also exist, see
Remark

8.15
and
Remark

8.14
.

Theorem D

(
Theorem

8.16
)
.

Let
X
be a quasiexcellent finite-dimensional scheme which is proper over a noetherian ring
R
. Let
\mathcal{Y}:\mathbf{D}^{-}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X),\operatorname{Mod}(R))
be the restricted Yoneda functor. Then,

•

The restricted Yoneda functor
\mathcal{Y}
is full on
\mathbf{D}^{-}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)^{\operatorname{op}}
, and the essential image is all the homological functors
H:\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\to\operatorname{Mod}(R)
such that for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
,
H(\Sigma^{n}F)
is a finitely generated
R
-module for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and vanishes for
n>>0
.

•

The functor
\mathcal{Y}
gives an equivalence between
\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(X)
and all the homological functors
H:\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\to\operatorname{Mod}(R)
such that for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
,
H(\Sigma^{n}F)
is a finitely generated
R
-module for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and vanishes for
|n|>>0
.

2.
Background and notation

Throughout this work, we will use
\Sigma
to denote the autoequivalence in the definition of triangulated categories. For any functor
F:\mathsf{S}\to\mathsf{T}
,
F(\mathsf{S})
will denote the essential image of
\mathsf{S}
under
F
. Further, all the gradings involved in this work will be cohomological.

We begin by setting up some basic notation.

Notation 2.1
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category, with full subcategories
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{C}
. Then, we define the following full subcategories of
\mathsf{T}
,

•

\mathsf{A}^{\perp}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{c\in\mathsf{T}:\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(a,c)=0\text{ for all }a\in\mathsf{A}\}
.

•

Similarly,
{}^{\perp}\mathsf{A}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{c\in\mathsf{T}:\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(c,a)=0\text{ for all }a\in\mathsf{A}\}

•

If
\mathsf{C}\subseteq\mathsf{A}^{\perp}
, we write
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{C})=0
.

•

\mathsf{A}\star\mathsf{C}
is the full subcategories of all extensions of
\mathsf{A}
by
\mathsf{C}
, that is, it is given by objects
B
such that there exists a triangle
A\to B\to C\to\Sigma A\text{ with }A\in\mathsf{A}\text{ and }C\in\mathsf{C}

•

We define
\mathsf{A}^{\star n}
inductively.
\mathsf{A}^{\star 1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{A}^{\star(n+1)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{A}^{\star n}\star\mathsf{A}
.

It follows from the octahedral axiom that the
\star
operation is associative, that is, for any full subcategories
\mathsf{A}
,
\mathsf{B}
, and
\mathsf{C}
of
\mathsf{T}
, we have
\mathsf{A}\star(\mathsf{B}\star\mathsf{C})=(\mathsf{A}\star\mathsf{B})\star\mathsf{C}
.

We now recall some notation from
[
BvdB03
,
Nee21b
]
on generating full subcategories from a given set of objects.

Notation 2.2
.

[
Nee21b
, Reminder 0.8]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category, and
\mathsf{U}
a set of objects in
\mathsf{T}
. Then, we define the following full subcategories of
\mathsf{T}
,

•

\operatorname{smd}(\mathsf{U})
is the closure of
\mathsf{U}
under direct summands.

•

\operatorname{add}(\mathsf{U})
is the closure of
\mathsf{U}
under finite direct sums.

•

For integers
A\leq B
,
\mathsf{U}[A,B]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\Sigma^{-i}U:U\in\mathsf{U}\text{ and }A\leq i\leq B\}
. We extend this definition to
A=-\infty
and
B=\infty
in the natural way. For example, we have that
\mathsf{U}(-\infty,B]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\Sigma^{-i}U:U\in\mathsf{U}\text{ and }i\leq B\}
.

•

\operatorname{coprod}_{n}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])
is defined inductively. For
n=1
,
\operatorname{coprod}_{1}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{add}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])
. Then, we inductively define
\operatorname{coprod}_{n+1}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{coprod}_{1}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])\star\operatorname{coprod}_{n}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])
.

•

\operatorname{coprod}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])
is the closure of
\mathsf{U}[A,B]
under finite direct sums and extensions. Note that
\operatorname{coprod}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])=\bigcup_{n\geq 1}\operatorname{coprod}_{n}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])
.

•

For any
n\geq 0
, let
\langle\mathsf{U}\rangle^{[A,B]}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}(\operatorname{coprod}_{n}(\mathsf{U}[A,B]))
.

•

\langle\mathsf{U}\rangle^{[A,B]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}(\operatorname{coprod}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])
.

•

When
A=-\infty
and
B=\infty
in the previous definitions, we drop the superscript. That is,
\langle\mathsf{U}\rangle_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}(\operatorname{coprod}_{n}(\mathsf{U}(-\infty,\infty)))
and
\langle\mathsf{U}\rangle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}(\operatorname{coprod}(\mathsf{U}(-\infty,\infty)))
. Note that
\langle\mathsf{U}\rangle
is the thick triangulated category generated by
\mathsf{U}
.

If
\mathsf{T}
has coproducts, we can define the corresponding “big” categories too.

•

\operatorname{Add}(\mathsf{U})
is the closure of
\mathsf{U}
under arbitrary coproducts.

•

We first define
\operatorname{Coprod}_{1}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{Add}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])
. Then, we inductively define
\operatorname{Coprod}_{n+1}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{Coprod}_{1}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])\star\operatorname{Coprod}_{n}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])
.

•

\operatorname{Coprod}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])
is the closure of
\mathsf{U}[A,B]
under arbitrary coproducts and extensions.

•

For any
n\geq 0
,
\overline{\langle\mathsf{U}\rangle}_{n}^{[A,B]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}(\operatorname{Coprod}_{n}(\mathsf{U}[A,B]))
.

•

\overline{\langle U\rangle}^{[A,B]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}(\operatorname{Coprod}(\mathsf{U}[A,B])
.

•

When
A=-\infty
and
B=\infty
in the previous definition, we drop the superscript. That is,
\overline{\langle U\rangle}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}(\operatorname{Coprod}_{n}(\mathsf{U}(-\infty,\infty)))
and
\overline{\langle U\rangle}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}(\operatorname{Coprod}(\mathsf{U}(-\infty,\infty)))
.

Remark 2.3
.

In
Notation

2.2
, if
\mathsf{U}=\{G\}
for an object
G\in\mathsf{T}
, then we denote
\langle U\rangle
by
\langle G\rangle
, and similarly for the other categories defined in
Notation

2.2
. Further,

•

An object
G\in\mathsf{T}
is said to be a
classical generator
for
\mathsf{T}
if
\mathsf{T}=\langle G\rangle
.

•

An object
G\in\mathsf{T}
is said to be a
strong generator
for
\mathsf{T}
if
\mathsf{T}=\langle G\rangle_{n}
for some
n\geq 1
.

•

The
Rouquier dimension
of a triangulated category
\mathsf{T}
is the infimum over
n
such that there exists an object
G\in\mathsf{T}
with
\mathsf{T}=\langle G\rangle_{n+1}
, see
[
Rou08
, Definition 3.2]
.

We recall the notions of t-structures and co-t-structures now, mostly to fix the notation.

Definition 2.4
.

[
BBD82
, Définition 1.3.1]
Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category. A t-structure on
\mathsf{T}
is a pair of strictly full subcategories
(\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0},\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0})
such that,

(1)

\Sigma\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}\subseteq\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
and
\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}\subseteq\Sigma\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
.

(2)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0},\Sigma^{-1}\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0})=0
.

(3)

For all
t\in\mathsf{T}
, there exists a triangle
u\to t\to v\to\Sigma u
with
u\in\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
and
v\in\Sigma^{-1}\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
.

By
[
BBD82
, Proposition 1.3.3]
, the triangle in (3) is unique.

We define
\mathsf{T}^{\leq n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Sigma^{-n}\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
and
\mathsf{T}^{\geq n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Sigma^{-n}\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
.

We shall sometimes also denote a t-structure by the pair
(\mathsf{U},\mathsf{V})
, with
\mathsf{U}=\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
and
\mathsf{V}=\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
. Further, we define the
heart of the t-structure
to be
\mathsf{T}^{\heartsuit}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}\cap\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
. Note that the heart of any t-structure is an abelian category, see
[
BBD82
, Théorème 1.3.6]
.

The following is an important method of constructing t-structures.

Theorem 2.5
.

[
ATJLSS03
, Theorem A.1]
Let
\mathsf{C}
be a set of compact objects in a triangulated category
\mathsf{T}
with coproducts. Then,
(\operatorname{Coprod}(\cup_{i\geq 0}\Sigma^{i}\mathsf{C}),(\cup_{i\geq 1}\Sigma^{i}\mathsf{C})^{\perp})
is a t-structure on
\mathsf{T}
, see
Notation

2.2
. We call this t-structure the
t-structure generated by
\mathsf{C}
, and such t-structures are called
compactly generated t-structures
.

We now define a co-t-structure or a weight structure. This notion was defined independently by Paukztello and Bondarko around the same time.

Definition 2.6
.

[
Bon10
, Definition 1.1.1]
and
[
Pau08
, Definition 1.4]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category. A co-t-structure on
\mathsf{T}
is a pair of strictly full subcategories
(\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0},\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0})
closed under direct summands such that,

(1)

\Sigma\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}\subseteq\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
and
\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}\subseteq\Sigma\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
.

(2)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0},\Sigma\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0})=0
.

(3)

For all
t\in\mathsf{T}
, there exists a triangle
u\to t\to v\to\Sigma u
with
u\in\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
and
v\in\Sigma\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
.

We define
\mathsf{T}^{\leq n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Sigma^{-n}\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
and
\mathsf{T}^{\geq n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Sigma^{-n}\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
. Further, we define the
heart of the co-t-structure
to be
\mathsf{T}^{\heartsuit}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}\cap\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
.

We shall sometimes also denote a co-t-structure by the pair
(\mathsf{U},\mathsf{V})
, with
\mathsf{U}=\mathsf{T}^{\geq 0}
and
\mathsf{V}=\mathsf{T}^{\leq 0}
.

Theorem 2.7
.

[
Pau12
, Theorem 5]
Let
\mathsf{C}
be a set of compact objects in a triangulated category
\mathsf{T}
with coproducts, such that
\Sigma^{-1}\mathsf{C}\subseteq\mathsf{C}
. Let
\mathsf{V}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Sigma^{-1}\mathsf{C}^{\perp}
. Then,
(^{\perp}(\Sigma\mathsf{V}),\mathsf{V})
is a co-t-structure on
\mathsf{T}
. Such co-t-structures are called
compactly generated co-t-structures
.

Recall that a collection of maps
\mathcal{I}
is called an ideal if the sum of two maps in
\mathcal{I}
is also in
\mathcal{I}
, and for any triple of composable maps
g
,
f
, and
h
, the map
hfg
is in
\mathcal{I}
if
f
is in
\mathcal{I}
.
We now recall some notions and results from
[
Chr98
]
, as we will need them in
Section

6
.

Definition 2.8
.

[
Chr98
, Definition 2.5]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category.
Now, let
\mathcal{P}
be a collection of objects, and
\mathcal{J}
a collection of maps in
\mathsf{T}
. Then, we define,

(1)

null
(\mathcal{P})=\{f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(X,Y):X,Y\in\mathsf{T}\text{ and }fg=0\text{ for all }g\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{P},X)\}

(2)

proj
(\mathcal{J})=\{P\in\mathsf{T}:(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(P,X)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(-,f)}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(P,Y))=0\text{ for all }f\in\mathcal{J}\}

(3)

(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{J})
is a
projective class
if
\mathcal{J}=\textbf{null}(\mathcal{P})
and
\mathcal{P}=\textbf{proj}(\mathcal{J})
and every object
X\in\mathsf{T}
admits a triangle
P\to X\xrightarrow{f}Y\to\Sigma P
with
P\in\mathcal{P}
and
f\in\mathcal{J}
.

Definition 2.9
.

[
Chr98
, Section 5]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with coproducts. A map
f:X\to Y
is called a
phantom map
if for any compact object
K
, and any map
K\to X
, the composite
K\to X\xrightarrow{f}Y
vanishes. Note that the collection of phantom maps forms an ideal
\mathcal{I}
. Then, if
\big(\overline{\langle\mathsf{T}^{c}\rangle}_{1},\mathcal{I}\big)
is a projective class, it is called the
phantom projective class
, see
Notation

2.2
. This is always the case if
\mathsf{T}
has all coproducts, and
\mathsf{T}^{c}
is essentially small. As we noted before, this is in particular the case if
\mathsf{T}
is compactly generated.

Theorem 2.10
.

[
Chr98
, Theorem 1.1]

Let
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{J})
be a projective class. Define
\mathcal{P}_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
inductively by
\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{P}_{n+1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}(\mathcal{P}_{n}\star\mathcal{P}_{1})
. Then,
(\mathcal{P}_{n},\mathcal{J}^{n})
is a projective class for all
n\geq 1
.

2.1.
Metrics and completions for triangulated categories

In this subsection we will go over the definitions and results we need related to metrics and completions of triangulated categories à la Neeman. See
[
Nee20
]
for a
discussion on how the notion of good metrics relates to the notion of metrics on a category as introduced in
[
Law73
, Page 139-140]
. All of the results in this subsection are from
[
Nee18a
]
and
[
Nee20
]
.

Definition 2.11
.

[
Nee20
, Definition 10]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category. A
good metric
is a sequence
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
of strictly full subcategories containing 0 such that,

(1)

\mathcal{M}_{n}\star\mathcal{M}_{n}=\mathcal{M}_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
.

(2)

\Sigma^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{n+1}\cup\mathcal{M}_{n+1}\cup\Sigma\mathcal{M}_{n+1}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
.

Remark 2.12
.

In
Definition

2.11
, the intuition to keep in mind is that
\mathcal{M}_{n}
represents the ball of radius
2^{-n}
around
0
. To be more precise, given a good metric
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
, one can define the length of a morphism
f
to be
2^{-N_{f}}
where we define
N_{f}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sup\{n:\operatorname{Cone}(f)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\}
. In fact, this does give a metric in the sense of Lawvere, see
[
Nee20
, Heuristic 9]
.

Definition 2.13
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category. A good metric
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
on
\mathsf{T}
is said to be
finer
than a good metric
\{\mathcal{N}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
if for every
i\geq 1
, there exists
j\geq 1
such that
\mathcal{M}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{i}
. We say the metrics
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{\mathcal{N}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
are
equivalent
if
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
is finer than
\{\mathcal{N}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{\mathcal{N}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
is also finer than
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}

Definition 2.14
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with a good metric
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
. A
Cauchy sequence
is a sequence
E_{1}\to E_{2}\to E_{3}\to E_{4}\to\cdots
in
\mathsf{T}
such that for each
i\geq 1
, there exists
n\geq 1
such that
\operatorname{Cone}(E_{j}\to E_{j+1})\in\mathcal{M}_{i}
for all
j\geq n
. Note that given a Cauchy sequence of the form above, there exists a subsequence
E^{\prime}_{1}\to E^{\prime}_{2}\to E^{\prime}_{3}\to E^{\prime}_{4}\to\cdots
such that
\operatorname{Cone}(E^{\prime}_{i}\to E^{\prime}_{i+1})\in\mathcal{M}_{i+1}
for each
i\geq 1
.

Definition 2.15
.

[
Nee18a
, Definition 1.10]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be an essentially small triangulated category with a good metric
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
. We denote the Yoneda embedding by
\mathcal{Y}:\mathsf{T}\to\operatorname{Mod}(\mathsf{T})
, where
\operatorname{Mod}(\mathsf{T})
denote the functor category
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathsf{T}^{\operatorname{op}},\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbb{Z}))
. Then, we can define the following full subcategories of
\operatorname{Mod}(\mathsf{T})
,

(1)

\mathfrak{L}(\mathsf{T})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits\mathcal{Y}(E_{i}):E_{1}\to E_{2}\to\cdots\text{ is a Cauchy sequence in }\mathsf{T}\}
.

(2)

\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{T})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{n\geq 1}\mathcal{Y}(\mathcal{M}_{n})^{\perp}
.

(3)

\mathfrak{S}(\mathsf{T})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathfrak{L}(\mathsf{T})\cap\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{T})
. This is a triangulated category by
[
Nee18a
, Theorem 2.11]

We note here that equivalent metrics (
Definition

2.13
) give rise to the same
\mathfrak{L}(\mathsf{T})
,
\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{T})
, and
\mathfrak{S}(\mathsf{T})
.

The computation of the
\mathfrak{S}(\mathsf{T})
is difficult in general. The following result, which was proven in a more general setting in
[
Nee18a
]
, helps us in the computation in a special situation.

Theorem 2.16
.

[
Nee18a
, Theorem 3.15]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be triangulated category with coproducts and let
\mathsf{T}^{c}
be the full subcategory of compact objects. Assume
\mathsf{T}^{c}
has a good metric
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
. Let
\mathcal{Y}:\mathsf{T}\to\operatorname{Hom}([\mathsf{T}^{c}]^{\operatorname{op}},\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbb{Z}))
be the restricted Yoneda functor. Then, we can define the following full subcategories of
\mathsf{T}
,

(1)

\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{F\in\mathsf{T}:F\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\underset{\boldsymbol{\xrightarrow{\hskip 24.18501pt}}}{{Hocolim}}}E_{n}\text{ for a Cauchy sequence }E_{1}\to E_{2}\to\cdots\text{ in }\mathsf{T}^{c}\}
.

(2)

\mathcal{Y}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{T}^{c}))=\bigcup_{n\geq 1}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\perp}
, where the orthogonal is taken in
\mathsf{T}
, see
Definition

2.15
(2).

(3)

\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})\cap\mathcal{Y}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{T}^{c}))

Then, the obvious functor from
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})\to\mathfrak{L}(\mathsf{T}^{c})
is an essential surjection which restricts to an equivalence
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})\to\mathfrak{S}(\mathsf{T}^{c})
, see
Definition

2.15
.

2.2.
Approximable triangulated categories

In this subsection, we recall the definition of an approximable triangulated category, and review some related notions and results. All of the definitions and results are from
[
Nee21b
]
. We begin by recalling an equivalence relation on the collection of t-structures on a triangulated category.

Definition 2.17
.

[
Nee21b
, Definition 0.10]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category. Then, two t-structures
(\mathsf{U},\mathsf{V})
and
(\mathsf{U}^{\prime},\mathsf{V}^{\prime})
are said to be equivalent if there exists an integer
n\geq 0
such that,

\Sigma^{n}\mathsf{U}\subseteq\mathsf{U}^{\prime}\subseteq\Sigma^{-n}\mathsf{U}

This is an equivalence relation on the collection of t-structures on
\mathsf{T}
.

Definition 2.18
.

[
Nee21b
, Definition 0.14]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with a single compact generator
G
. Then, we define,

(1)

The
t-structure generated by
G
to be
(\mathsf{T}_{G}^{\leq 0},\mathsf{T}_{G}^{\geq 0})
, where
\mathsf{T}_{G}^{\leq 0}=\operatorname{Coprod}\big(\{\Sigma^{i}G\}_{i\geq 0}\big)
and
\mathsf{T}_{G}^{\geq 0}=\big(\Sigma\mathsf{T}_{G}^{\leq 0}\big)^{\perp}
, see
Notation

2.2
. This is a t-structure by
Theorem

2.5
.

(2)

The
preferred equivalence class of t-structures
is the equivalence class of t-structures which contains the t-structure
(\mathsf{T}_{G}^{\leq 0},\mathsf{T}_{G}^{\geq 0})
, under the equivalence relation of
Definition

2.17
.

Definition 2.19
.

[
Nee21b
, Definition 0.16]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with coproducts and let
(\mathsf{U},\mathsf{V})
be a t-structure on
\mathsf{T}
. Then, we define,

(1)

\mathsf{T}^{-}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathsf{U}
,
\mathsf{T}^{+}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathsf{V}
and
\mathsf{T}^{b}=\mathsf{T}^{-}\cap\mathsf{T}^{+}

(2)

\mathsf{T}^{-}_{c}=\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big(\mathsf{T}^{c}\star\Sigma^{n}\mathsf{U}\big)

(3)

\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}=\mathsf{T}^{-}_{c}\cap\mathsf{T}^{b}

Note that all of these categories remain the same if we replace the t-structure by an equivalent one. If
\mathsf{T}
has a single compact generator, we will always compute these categories with respect to a t-structure in the preferred equivalence class unless explicitly stated otherwise.

We now recall the definition of approximability.

Definition 2.20
.

[
Nee21b
, Definition 0.21]

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with a single compact generator
G
. Then, we say that
\mathsf{T}
is
weakly approximable
if there exists a positive integer
A
and a t-structure
(\mathsf{U},\mathsf{V})
such that,

(1)

\Sigma^{A}G\in\mathsf{U}
.

(2)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\Sigma^{-i}G,\mathsf{U})=0
for all
i\geq A
.

(3)

For all
F\in\mathsf{U}
, there exists a triangle
E\to F\to D\to\Sigma E
with
E\in\overline{\langle G\rangle}^{[-A,A]}
and
D\in\Sigma\mathsf{U}
, see
Notation

2.2
.

We say that
\mathsf{T}
is
approximable
, if further,

(3)
′

For all
F\in\mathsf{U}
, there exists a triangle
E\to F\to D\to\Sigma E
with
E\in\overline{\langle G\rangle}^{[-A,A]}_{A}
and
D\in\Sigma\mathsf{U}
, see
Notation

2.2
.

It is easy to verify that if
\mathsf{T}
is weakly approximable (resp. approximable) with respect to one compact generator, then it is weakly approximable (resp. approximable) with respect to any other compact generator up to increasing the integer
A
if necessary, see
[
Nee21b
, Proposition 2.6]
.

2.3.
On categories coming from algebraic geometry

We start by fixing the notation for some abelian categories arising from algebra and algebraic geometry.

Notation 2.21
.

Let
R
be a ring. Then,

(1)

\operatorname{Mod}(R)
denotes the category of (right)
R
-modules.

(2)

If
R
is a coherent ring,
\mathbf{mod}(R)
denotes the category of finitely presented modules. In particular, if the ring is noetherian, these coincide with the category of finitely generated modules.

Notation 2.22
.

Let
X
be a quasicompact and quasiseparated scheme, and
Z\subseteq X
a closed subset with a quasicompact complement. Then,

(1)

\operatorname{Qcoh}(X)
is the abelian category of all quasicoherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.

(2)

\operatorname{Qcoh}_{Z}(X)
is the abelian category of all quasicoherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules with support contained in the closed subset
Z
.

If
X
is further a noetherian scheme, we also define the following categories,

(3)

\operatorname{coh}(X)
is the abelian category of all coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.

(4)

\operatorname{coh}_{Z}(X)
is the abelian category of all coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules with support contained in the closed subset
Z
.

The primary examples and applications in this text will be to triangulated categories arising from algebraic geometry. Below we list some of the important ones, both as a reminder, and also to fix the notation.

Notation 2.23
.

Let
X
be a quasicompact and quasiseparated scheme, and
Z\subseteq X
a closed subset with a quasicompact complement. Then,

(1)

\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(X)
denotes the derived category of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules with quasicoherent cohomology. It is a compactly generated triangulated category, with the subcategory of compact objects being exactly
\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(X)
defined below, see
[
Nee24
, Theorem 3.2(i)]
.

(2)

\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(X)
denotes the derived category of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules with quasicoherent cohomology which are supported on
Z
. That is, the restriction to
\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(U)
is isomorphic to 0, where
U
is the complement of
Z
in
X
. It is also a compactly generated triangulated category, with the subcategory of compact objects being exactly
\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}_{Z}(X)
defined below, see
[
Nee24
, Theorem 3.2(i)]
.

(3)

\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(X)\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(X)
denotes the strictly full subcategory of perfect complexes. If
X=\operatorname{Spec}(R)
is an affine scheme, then there is an equivalence
\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(X)\cong\mathbf{K}^{b}(\operatorname{proj}\text{-}R)
, that is, the bounded homotopy category of finitely generated projective modules. Throughout this document, we will confuse
\mathbf{K}^{b}(\operatorname{proj}\text{-}R)
with its essential image in
\mathbf{D}(R)
.

(4)

\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}_{Z}(X)\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)
denotes the strictly full subcategory of perfect complexes with cohomologies supported on
Z
.

(5)

\mathbf{D}^{p}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(X)
denotes the strictly full subcategory of pseudocoherent complexes supported on
Z
. A complex is pseudocoherent if its restriction to the derived category of any affine open subscheme is isomorphic to a bounded above complex of finitely generated projective modules.

(6)

\mathbf{D}^{p,b}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)\subseteq\mathbf{D}^{p}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)
denotes the strictly full subcategory of cohomologically bounded pesudeocoherent complexes supported on
Z
.

(7)

\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))
denotes the derived category of quasicoherent sheaves on
X
. If
X
is separated, then
\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))
is equivalent to
\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(X)
.

If
X
is further a noetherian scheme, then,

(8)

\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(X)
denotes the strictly full subcategory of cohomologically bounded complexes of sheaves
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules with coherent cohomology. Note that if
X
is separated,
\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
is equivalent to
\mathbf{D^{b}}(\operatorname{coh}(X))\subseteq\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))
, which is the full subcategory of cohomologically bounded complexes of coherent sheaves.

(9)

Similarly,
\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh},Z}(X)\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)
denotes the strictly full subcategory of cohomologically bounded complexes of sheaves
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules with coherent cohomology. Note that it agrees with
\mathbf{D}^{p,b}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)
.

(10)

\mathbf{D}^{+}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
denotes the derived category of sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules with bounded below and coherent cohomology.

(11)

\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
denotes the homotopy category of injective quasicoherent sheaves. It is a compactly generated triangulated category. The subcategory of compact objects is equivalent to
\mathbf{D^{b}}(\operatorname{coh}(X))
via the functor sending a complex in
\mathbf{D^{b}}(\operatorname{coh}(X))
to its injective resolution, see
[
Kra05
, Proposition 2.3]
.

(12)

If
X
is separated,
\mathbf{K}_{m}(\operatorname{Proj}\text{-}X)
denotes the mock homotopy category of projectives, see
[
Mur08
]
for details.

Theorem 2.24
.

[
Nee21b
, Example 3.4]

Let
X
be a quasicompact and quasiseparated scheme, and
Z\subseteq X
a closed subset with quasicompact complement. Then,
\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)
is weakly approximable, see
Definition

2.20
. Further,

\big(\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)\big)^{-}_{c}=\mathbf{D}^{p}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)\text{ and }\big(\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)\big)^{b}_{c}=\mathbf{D}^{p,b}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)

see
Definition

2.19
.
Finally, from
[
Nee18a
, Proposition 0.15(i)]
, we get that,

\mathfrak{S}(\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}_{Z}(X))=\mathbf{D}^{p,b}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)

with respect to the metric on
\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}_{Z}(X)
given by
\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}_{Z}(X)\cap\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(X)^{\leq-n}\}_{n\geq 1}
. See
[
Nee18a
, Remark 4.7]
for other equivalent metrics.
We recall here that
\mathbf{D}^{p,b}_{\operatorname{Qcoh},Z}(X)=\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
for any noetherian scheme
X
.

3.
Generating sequence and orthogonal metrics

Recall that a good metric is
\mathbb{N}
-graded, see
Definition

2.11
. In this work, we will require metrics to be
\mathbb{Z}
-graded at times, instead of
\mathbb{N}
-graded. We give the definition below.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
\mathsf{S}
be a triangulated category. An
extended good metric
on
\mathsf{S}
is a sequence of strictly full subcategories
\mathcal{M}=\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
each containing 0, such that,

(1)

\mathcal{M}_{n}\star\mathcal{M}_{n}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

\Sigma\mathcal{M}_{n+1}\cup\mathcal{M}_{n+1}\cup\Sigma^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{n+1}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

We say an extended good metric is an
orthogonal metric
if
{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\perp})=\mathcal{M}_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Note that if
\mathsf{S}
has coproducts and
\mathcal{M}
is an orthogonal metric, then
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is closed under coproducts for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Remark 3.2
.

Note that every good metric
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
can be made into an extended good metric by setting
\mathcal{M}_{-n}=\mathsf{S}
for all
n\geq 0
. Conversely, if
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
is an extended good metric, then
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
is a good metric. So, from now on, when we write metric, we would mean an extended good metric, or a good metric, depending on the context. Observe that some notions only depend on the
\mathcal{M}_{n}
for
n>>0
, for example the notion of a Cauchy sequence, see
Definition

2.14
.

We extend the definition of equivalence of metrics to extended good metrics in the obvious manner, see
[
Nee20
, Definition 2]
. Further, we introduce the notion of
\mathbb{N}
-equivalence of extended good metrics.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
\mathsf{S}
be a triangulated category. Then,

(1)

We say an extended good metric
\mathcal{M}
is
finer
than an extended good metric
\mathcal{N}
if for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there exists
j\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\mathcal{M}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{i}
. We denote this by
\mathcal{M}\preceq\mathcal{N}
.

(2)

We say an extended good metric
\mathcal{M}
is
\mathbb{N}
-
finer
than an extended good metric
\mathcal{N}
if there exists
n\geq 0
such that
\mathcal{M}_{i+n}\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{i}
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. We denote this by
\mathcal{M}\preceq_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{N}
.

see
Definition

3.1
for the definition of an extended good metric.
We say that
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{N}
are equivalent if
\mathcal{M}\preceq\mathcal{N}\preceq\mathcal{M}
. Similarly, we say that they are
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent if
\mathcal{M}\preceq_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{N}\preceq_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{M}
.

The two notions coincide in some cases, for example when the metrics arise from t-structures or co-t-structures. The following remark states this in a slightly more general setting.

Remark 3.4
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category, with extended good metrics
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{N}
(
Definition

3.1
) such that one of the following two conditions hold,

(1)

There exist two strictly full subcategories
\mathsf{U}_{1}
and
\mathsf{U}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{M}_{n}=\Sigma^{n}\mathsf{U}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{N}_{n}=\Sigma^{n}\mathsf{U}_{2}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

There exist two strictly full subcategories
\mathsf{U}_{1}
and
\mathsf{U}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{M}_{n}=\Sigma^{-n}\mathsf{U}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{N}_{n}=\Sigma^{-n}\mathsf{U}_{2}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Then,
\mathcal{M}\preceq\mathcal{N}
if and only if
\mathcal{M}\preceq_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{N}
, see
Definition

3.3
.

We now introduce the notion of a pre-generating sequence.

Definition 3.5
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a compactly generated triangulated category. A
pre-generating sequence
is a sequence of full subcategories
\{\mathcal{G}^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
of
\mathsf{T}^{c}
such that the smallest triangulated subcategory of
\mathsf{T}
containing the union of
\mathcal{G}^{i}
which is closed under finite direct sums, extensions, and summands is exactly
\mathsf{T}^{c}
. In other words,
\operatorname{smd}(\operatorname{coprod}(\cup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{G}^{i}))=\mathsf{T}^{c}
, see
Notation

2.2
. Note that this implies that
\big(\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{G}^{i}\big)^{\perp}=0
as
\mathsf{T}
is compactly generated.

Given a pre-generating sequence, we define some full subcategories of
\mathsf{T}
in a similar spirit to
Notation

2.2
.

Notation 3.6
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a compactly generated triangulated category with a pre-generating sequence
\mathcal{G}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\mathcal{G}^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
, see
Definition

3.5
. We define the following full subcategories of
\mathsf{T}
for any integer
i\leq j
,

•

\mathcal{G}{[i,j]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{i\leq n\leq j}\mathcal{G}^{n}

•

\mathcal{G}{(-\infty,j]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{n\leq j}\mathcal{G}^{n}

•

\mathcal{G}{[i,\infty)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{i\leq n}\mathcal{G}^{n}

•

\mathcal{G}{(-\infty,\infty)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{G}^{n}

And the following full subcategories of
\mathsf{T}
for any integer
i\leq j
and
n\geq 1
,

•

\mathcal{G}^{[i,j]}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{coprod}_{n}\left(\mathcal{G}{[i,j]}\right)\right)

•

\mathcal{G}^{(-\infty,j]}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{coprod}_{n}\left(\mathcal{G}{(-\infty,j]}\right)\right)

•

\mathcal{G}^{[i,\infty)}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{coprod}_{n}\left(\mathcal{G}{[i,\infty)}\right)\right)

•

\mathcal{G}^{[i,j]}_{\infty}=\mathcal{G}^{[i,j]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{coprod}\left(\mathcal{G}{[i,j]}\right)\right)

•

\mathcal{G}^{(-\infty,j]}_{\infty}=\mathcal{G}^{(-\infty,j]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{coprod}\left(\mathcal{G}{(-\infty,j]}\right)\right)

•

\mathcal{G}^{[i,\infty)}_{\infty}=\mathcal{G}^{[i,\infty)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{coprod}\left(\mathcal{G}{[i,\infty)}\right)\right)

•

\mathcal{G}_{n}=\mathcal{G}^{(-\infty,\infty)}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{coprod}\left(\mathcal{G}(-\infty,\infty)\right)\right)

see
Notation

2.2
.
We also define the analogous big categories. For any integer
i\leq j
and
n\geq 1
,

•

\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{[i,j]}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{Coprod}_{n}\left(\mathcal{G}{[i,j]}\right)\right)

•

\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{(-\infty,j]}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{Coprod}_{n}\left(\mathcal{G}{(-\infty,j]}\right)\right)

•

\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{[i,\infty)}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{Coprod}_{n}\left(\mathcal{G}{[i,\infty)}\right)\right)

•

\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{[i,j]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{Coprod}\left(\mathcal{G}{[i,j]}\right)\right)

•

\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{(-\infty,j]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{Coprod}\left(\mathcal{G}{(-\infty,j]}\right)\right)

•

\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{[i,\infty)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{smd}\left(\operatorname{Coprod}\left(\mathcal{G}{[i,\infty)}\right)\right)

Remark 3.7
.

We note here that for a pre-generating sequence (
Definition

3.5
), we recover the small categories from the big ones on taking the intersection with the compacts by
[
Nee21a
, Proposition 1.9]
. That is,
\overline{\mathcal{G}}^{?}_{!}\cap\mathsf{T}^{c}=\mathcal{G}^{?}_{!}
for any choice of
?
and
!
as in
Notation

3.6
.

We now give the definition of a generating sequence.

Definition 3.8
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a compactly generated triangulated category. A
generating sequence
is a sequence of full subcategories
\mathcal{G}=\{\mathcal{G}^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
of
\mathsf{T}^{c}
such that

(1)

\mathcal{G}
is a pre-generating sequence (
Definition

3.5
), that is,
\operatorname{smd}(\operatorname{coprod}(\cup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{G}^{i}))=\mathsf{T}^{c}
.

(2)

\Sigma^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{n}\cup\mathcal{G}^{n}\cup\Sigma\mathcal{G}^{n}\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{[n-1,n+1]}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, see
Notation

3.6
. In particular, we get an extended good metric (see
Definition

3.1
)
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
on
\mathsf{T}^{c}
with
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
defined as follows,

\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{G}^{(-\infty,-n]}

Finally, we call a generating sequence a
finite generating sequence
if,

(3)

Each of the subcategories
\mathcal{G}^{i}
have only finitely many objects.

Given any generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
, we define the orthogonal to
\mathcal{G}
to be the full subcategory of
\mathsf{T}
given by
\mathcal{G}^{\perp}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{n})^{\perp}
. We define the extended good metric
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
on
\mathsf{T}
by,

\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{}^{\perp}\big[\big({\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\mathcal{G}}}\big)^{\perp}\big]\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{Z}

where the orthogonals are taken in
\mathsf{T}
. We show below that
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
is an orthogonal metric on
\mathsf{T}
, see
Definition

3.1
.

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category.
Then,

(1)

Let
\mathcal{M}\subseteq\mathsf{T}
be a full subcategory. We define
\mathcal{R}={}^{\perp}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\perp}\big)
. Then,
{}^{\perp}\big(\mathcal{R}^{\perp}\big)=\mathcal{R}
.

(2)

If
\mathsf{T}
has a generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
, then,
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
is an orthogonal good metric on
\mathsf{T}
, see
Definition

3.8
for the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
and
Definition

3.1
for the definition of an orthogonal metric.

Proof.

We just need to show (1), as it implies (2). For any full subcategory
\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathsf{T}
, we have the following inclusions,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({}^{\perp}\mathcal{N})^{\perp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{N}^{\perp})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i(\mathcal{N})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j(\mathcal{N})}

Note that taking the orthogonal of
i(\mathcal{N})
gives us the inclusion
l(\mathcal{N}):{}^{\perp}\big([{}^{\perp}\mathcal{N}]^{\perp}\big)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{}^{\perp}\mathcal{N}
.
This immediately gives us,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{R}^{\perp})={}^{\perp}\big([\mathcal{{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{M}^{\perp})]}^{\perp}\big)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{R}={}^{\perp}(\mathcal{M}^{\perp})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}^{\perp}\big([\mathcal{{}^{\perp}(\mathcal{M}^{\perp})]}^{\perp}\big)={}^{\perp}(\mathcal{R}^{\perp})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l(\mathcal{M}^{\perp})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j({}^{\perp}[\mathcal{M}^{\perp}])}

∎

The trivial example of a generating sequence would of course be to let each
\mathcal{G}^{i}=\mathsf{T}^{c}
. But non-trivial generating sequences also frequently occur naturally as the following example shows.

Example 3.10
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with a single compact generator
G
, then, the following are finite generating sequences (
Definition

3.8
),

(1)

\mathcal{G}^{i}=\{\Sigma^{i}{G}\}
for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

\mathcal{G}^{i}=\{\Sigma^{-i}{G}\}
for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

If there is a countable generating set
\{G_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, then we have the following finite generating sequence,

(3)

\mathcal{G}^{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\Sigma^{i-j}G_{j}:j\text{ lies between 0 and }i\}

We record the following easy lemma for later use.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with a generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
, see
Definition

3.8
. Suppose there exists an integer
A>0
such that for each integer
i
, we have that,
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i}\subseteq\bigcup_{|n|\leq A}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i+1}
, see
Definition

3.8
(2) for the definition of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
. Then,

(1)

For any integers
i
and positive integer
j
, we have that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i}\subseteq\bigcup_{|n|\leq jA}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i+j}
.

(2)

For any integer
i
,
\mathsf{T}^{c}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i}
.

Proof.

We prove (1) first. Fix an integer
i
. We will prove the claim by induction on
j\geq 1
. The base case
j=1
is true from the hypothesis. Suppose we know the result up to some positive integer
j
. So, we have that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i}\subseteq\bigcup_{|n|\leq jA}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i+j}
. Again, by the hypothesis, we have that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i+j}\subseteq\bigcup_{|n|\leq A}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i+j+1}
. So, we get that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i}\subseteq\bigcup_{|n|\leq(j+1)A}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i+j+1}
, which is what we needed for induction.

Now we prove (2). Fix an integer
i
. By
Definition

3.8
(2),
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i}\supseteq\bigcup_{j\geq i}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{j}
. Further, by (1), we have that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\subseteq\bigcup_{|n|\leq(i-j)A}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i}
for any
j\leq i
. And so,

\mathsf{T}^{c}=\mathcal{G}^{(-\infty,\infty)}=\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{G}^{(-\infty,-j]}=\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{j}\subseteq\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i}

with notation as in
Notation

3.6
.
∎

We now define a preorder, and a corresponding equivalence relation on the collection of generating sequences, which restricts to a preorder and an equivalence relation on the subcollection of finite generating sequences too, see
Definition

3.8
.

Definition 3.12
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a compactly generated triangulated category. We say that a generating sequence
\mathcal{G}_{1}
is
\mathbb{N}
-
finer
than
\mathcal{G}_{2}
if there exists a integer
N\geq 0
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{n}\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{2}^{[n-N,n+N]}
, see
Notation

3.6
. We denote this by
\mathcal{G}_{1}\preceq_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{G}_{2}
. We say two generating sequences
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{2}
are
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent if
\mathcal{G}_{1}\preceq_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{G}_{2}\preceq_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{G}_{1}
.

Example 3.13
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with a compact generator
G
. Suppose
H
is another compact generator for
\mathsf{T}
. This implies that,

\langle G\rangle=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\langle G\rangle^{[-n,n]}=\mathsf{T}^{c}=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\langle H\rangle^{[-n,n]}=\langle H\rangle

see
Notation

2.2
. And hence
H\in\langle G\rangle^{[-n,n]}
and
G\in\langle H\rangle^{[-n,n]}
for some integer
n\geq 0
. This immediately gives us that,

(1)

The generating sequence (
Definition

3.8
)
\mathcal{G}_{G}
given by
\mathcal{G}_{G}^{i}=\{\Sigma^{-i}G\}
is
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent (see
Definition

3.12
) to the generating sequence
\mathcal{G}_{H}
given by
\mathcal{G}_{H}^{i}=\{\Sigma^{-i}H\}
.

(2)

The generating sequence (
Definition

3.8
)
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}_{G}
given by
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{G}}^{i}=\{\Sigma^{i}G\}
is
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent to the generating sequence
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}_{H}
given by
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}_{H}^{i}=\{\Sigma^{i}H\}
.

We now show that
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent generating sequences give rise to
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent metrics.

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a compactly generated triangulated category with
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent generating sequences
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, see
Definition

3.12
for the definition of
\mathbb{N}
-equivalence of generating sequences. Then,

(1)

The corresponding extended good metrics
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
on
\mathsf{T}^{c}
are
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent, see
Definition

3.8
(2) for the definition of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
.

(2)

The corresponding orthogonal metrics
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
on
\mathsf{T}
are
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent, see
Definition

3.8
for the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
.

See
Definition

3.3
for the definition of
\mathbb{N}
-equivalence of extended good metrics

Proof.

By
Definition

3.12
, there exists an integer
N\geq 0
such that
\mathcal{G}^{\prime n}\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{[n-N,n+N]}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, see
Notation

3.6
. And so,
\mathcal{G}^{\prime(-\infty,-n]}\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{(-\infty,-n+N]}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. In particular, this shows that
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\preceq_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
. By symmetry, we get that they are in fact
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent

Further, this gives us that the right orthogonals have the reverse inclusion, that is,
(\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{n+N})^{\perp}\subseteq(\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{n})^{\perp}
. Taking left orthogonals, we get
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\preceq_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
. Again, by symmetry, we get that they are in fact
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent
∎

Inspired by this lemma, we can define the preferred equivalence class of orthogonal metrics for a triangulated category with a given generating sequence.

Definition 3.15
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a compactly generated triangulated category with a generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
, see
Definition

3.8
. Then,

(1)

An orthogonal metric
\mathcal{R}
lies in the
preferred equivalence class of orthogonal metrics
if it is equivalent to
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
.

(2)

An orthogonal metric
\mathcal{R}
lies in the
preferred
\mathbb{N}
-equivalence class of orthogonal metrics
if it is
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent to
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
.

See
Definition

3.8
for the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
,
Definition

3.1
for the definition of an orthogonal metric, and
Definition

3.3
for the definition of the equivalence relations on metrics. Recall from
Lemma

3.9
that
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
is an orthogonal metric.

Note that by
Lemma

3.14
the preferred equivalence class and the preferred
\mathbb{N}
-equivalence class of orthogonal metrics remain the same if
\mathcal{G}
is replaced by a
\mathbb{N}
-equivalent generating sequence, see
Definition

3.12
.

Remark 3.16
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a compactly generated triangulated category with a generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
, see
Definition

3.8
. Then,

(1)

If an orthogonal metric
\mathcal{R}
lies in the preferred equivalence class of orthogonal metrics then
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}\preceq\mathcal{R}\cap\mathsf{T}^{c}
.

(2)

If an orthogonal metric
\mathcal{R}
lies in the preferred
\mathbb{N}
-equivalence class of orthogonal metrics then
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}\preceq_{\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{R}\cap\mathsf{T}^{c}
.

See
Definition

3.3
for the definition of the order relation on metrics,
Definition

3.8
(2) for the definition of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
, and
Definition

3.1
for the definition of an orthogonal metric.

We now define the closure of the compacts with respect to a metric in the setup of a triangulated category with coproducts.

Definition 3.17
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with coproducts, let
\mathcal{R}
be an extended good metric (
Definition

3.1
) on
\mathsf{T}
, and
\mathcal{M}
be an extended good metric on the subcategory of compacts,
\mathsf{T}^{c}
. Then, the closure of the compacts with respect to
\mathcal{R}
is defined to be,

\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathsf{T}^{c}\star\mathcal{R}_{n}

We define the orthogonal to the metric
\mathcal{M}
to be,

\mathcal{M}^{\perp}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\perp}

where the orthogonal is taken in the category
\mathsf{T}
.
Finally, we define the bounded objects of the closure of the compacts to be,

\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}\cap\mathcal{M}^{\perp}

We are mostly interested in the case when
\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\perp}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{R}_{n}^{\perp}
.

Note that all of these categories remain the same if we replace
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{M}
with equivalent extended good metrics, see
Definition

3.3
. Unless mentioned otherwise, if we are working with a compactly generated triangulated category
\mathsf{T}
with a generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
, then we will compute the category
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
with respect to the metric
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
, see
Definition

3.8
. Further, we will define
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}
in this case by,
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}=\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}\cap\left(\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}\right)^{\perp}
. Note that
(\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}})^{\perp}=(\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}})^{\perp}
with both orthogonals being taken in
\mathsf{T}
.

Remark 3.18
.

The reason for calling
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
the closure of the compacts comes from metric spaces. Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with coproducts, and let
\mathcal{M}
be an extended good metric on the subcategory of compacts,
\mathsf{T}^{c}
. Then, any object
F
in
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
is an “arbitrarily small distance” from the category of compacts, as for any
n
, there exists a triangle
E\to F\to D\to\Sigma E
with
E
a compact object and
D
in
\mathcal{R}_{n}
. We recall from
Remark

2.12
that
\mathcal{R}_{n}
represents the ball of radius
2^{-n}
. Hence, the object
E
is less than
2^{-n}
distance away from
F
for all
n
. This exactly mimics the closure of a subspace of a metric space.

We now define the notion of a
\mathcal{G}
-preapproximable triangulated category, which will be a prequel to the notion of
\mathcal{G}
-approximability, which is the main new definition of this work. We also show that the closure of the compacts is thick for a
\mathcal{G}
-preapproximable triangulated category.

Definition 3.19
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a compactly generated triangulated category with a generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
(see
Definition

3.8
),

\mathcal{G}
-preapproximable
if there exists an orthogonal metric
\mathcal{R}
(see
Definition

3.1
), and an integer
A>0
such that,

(0)

For each integer
i
, we have that,
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i}\subseteq\bigcup_{|n|\leq A}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i+1}
.

(1)

\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i+A}\subseteq\mathcal{R}_{i}\cap\mathsf{T}^{c}
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{G}^{n},\mathcal{R}_{i})=0
for all
i>>0
.

We begin with a technical lemma which will be useful later.

Lemma 3.20
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a compactly generated triangulated category with a generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
(
Definition

3.8
), an orthogonal metric
\mathcal{R}
(
Definition

3.1
), and an integer
A>0
satisfying the conditions of
\mathcal{G}
-preapproximability, see
Definition

3.19
. Then,

(1)

\mathsf{T}^{c}\subseteq\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{R}_{i}
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

\mathsf{T}^{c}\subseteq\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}\subseteq\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{R}_{n}
, see
Definition

3.17
for the definition of
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
.

Proof.

By
Definition

3.19
(1),
\mathcal{R}_{i}\cap\mathsf{T}^{c}\supseteq\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i+A}
for all
i
. But, we have that

\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{R}_{i}\supseteq\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i+A}=\mathsf{T}^{c}

where the equality follows from
Lemma

3.11
(2) using
Definition

3.19
(0), which proves (1).

For (2), note that,

\mathsf{T}^{c}=\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{G}^{(-\infty,i]}=\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{i}\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{R}_{i-A}

see
Notation

3.6
and
Definition

3.8
(2) for the definition of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
.
This immediately implies that
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}=\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(\mathsf{T}^{c}\star\mathcal{R}_{n})\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{R}_{i}
.
∎

We now prove an easy lemma which will be useful to show that
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
is triangulated.

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with a pre-generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
(
Definition

3.5
), and an orthogonal metric
\mathcal{R}
(
Definition

3.1
) such that for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{G}^{n},\mathcal{R}_{i})=0
for all
i>>0
. Then,
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(H,\mathcal{R}_{i})=0
for all
i>>0
, for any compact object
H
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathsf{T}^{c}=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{G}^{[-n,n]}
(see
Notation

3.6
) as
\mathcal{G}
is a pre-generating sequence. And so, there exists
n>0
such that
H\in\mathcal{G}^{[-n,n]}
. From the hypothesis
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{G}[-n,n],\mathcal{R}_{i})=0
for all
i>>0
, and hence,
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{G}^{[-n,n]},\mathcal{R}_{i})=0
for all
i>>0
. This implies that
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{T}}(H,\mathcal{R}_{i})=0
for all
i>>0
, and we are done.
∎

Proposition 3.22
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a triangulated category with a pre-generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
, and an orthogonal metric
\mathcal{R}
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
