\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})=\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
.

Proof.

From
Lemma

6.2
(3), we have that,

\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})=\{F\in\mathsf{T}:F\text{ has a strong }\mathsf{T}^{c}\text{-approximating system}\}

noting that any
\mathsf{T}^{c}
-approximating system (
Definition

6.10
) is a Cauchy sequence (
Definition

2.14
) with respect to the metric
\mathcal{R}\cap\mathsf{T}^{c}
and conversely, any Cauchy sequence with respect to
\mathcal{R}\cap\mathsf{T}^{c}
has a subsequence which is a strong
\mathsf{T}^{c}
-approximating system.
Then, the result immediately follows from
Lemma

6.2
(1) and
Lemma

6.2
(3).
∎

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
a triangulated category with a generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
, see
Definition

3.8
. Let
\mathcal{Y}:\mathsf{T}\to\operatorname{Hom}([\mathsf{T}^{c}]^{\operatorname{op}},\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbb{Z}))
be the restricted Yoneda functor. Then,

(1)

If we equip
\mathsf{T}^{c}
with the metric
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
(see
Definition

3.8
), then,
\mathcal{Y}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{T}^{c}))=\big(\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}\big)^{\perp}
, see
Definition

3.17
for the definition of
\big(\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}\big)^{\perp}
and see
Definition

8.1
(2) for the definition of
\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{T}^{c})
.

(2)

If
\mathsf{T}
is weakly
\mathcal{G}
-quasiapproximable (
Definition

4.2
), and we equip
\mathsf{T}^{c}
with the metric
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}\cap\mathsf{T}^{c}
(see
Definition

3.8
), then also
\mathcal{Y}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{T}^{c}))=\big(\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}\big)^{\perp}
.

Proof.

(1)

Follows directly from
Theorem

2.16
(2).

(2)

As
\mathsf{T}
is weakly
\mathcal{G}
-quasiapproximable, the metrics
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}\cap\mathsf{T}^{c}
are equivalent by
Proposition

4.9
, and hence we are done by (1).

∎

Corollary 8.4
.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a weakly
\mathcal{G}
-quasiapproximable triangulated category (
Definition

4.2
). Consider
\mathsf{T}^{c}
with the extended good metric
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
, with
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}_{n}=\mathcal{G}^{(-\infty,-n]}
, see
Definition

3.8
and
Notation

3.6
. Then,
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})=\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
(
Definition

8.1
(1)), where the closure of the compacts
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
(see
Definition

3.17
) is with respect to the metric
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
, see
Definition

3.17
. Further, the category
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})=\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}
, see
Definition

8.1
(3) and
Definition

3.17
.

Proof.

For a weakly
\mathcal{G}
-quasiapproximable triangulated category, the metrics
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}\cap\mathsf{T}^{c}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
are equivalent by
Proposition

4.9
, see
Definition

3.8
for the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathcal{G}}
. So, the first part follows from
Proposition

8.2
. Further, by
Proposition

8.3
, we get that
\mathcal{Y}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{T}^{c}))=\left(\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}\right)^{\perp}
, see
Definition

3.17
. So,

\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}\cap\left(\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}\right)^{\perp}=\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})\cap\mathcal{Y}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{T}^{c}))=\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})

which is what we needed to show.
∎

Now, we do a few concrete computations. We start with the following lemma.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
X
be a noetherian scheme. Then the standard co-t-structure on
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
, which we denote by
(\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq 0},\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\leq 0})
, is compactly generated (see
Definition

7.2
). In fact,
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\leq 0}=\left(\bigcup_{n\geq 1}\Sigma^{-n}\operatorname{Coh}(X)\right)^{\perp}
.

Proof.

Consider the full subcategory
\operatorname{Coh}(X)\subseteq\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\subseteq\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
. We claim that the co-t-structure generated by
\operatorname{Coh}(X)
is exactly the standard co-t-structure. Let the co-t-structure generated by
\operatorname{Coh}(X)
be
(\mathsf{U},\mathsf{V})
. That is,
\mathsf{V}=\left(\bigcup_{n\geq 1}\Sigma^{-n}\operatorname{Coh}(X)\right)^{\perp}
and
\mathsf{U}={}^{\perp}\left(\Sigma\mathsf{V}\right)
It is enough to show that
\mathsf{V}=\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\leq 0}
.

Note that
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\leq 0}\subseteq\left(\bigcup_{n\geq 1}\Sigma^{-n}\operatorname{Coh}(X)\right)^{\perp}=\mathsf{V}
, by
[
Kra05
, Lemma 2.1]
. So, we just need to show the other inclusion. Let
F\in\mathsf{V}
. Then, first of all note that if any of the cohomology sheaves
\mathcal{H}^{n}(X)\neq 0
for some
n\geq 1
, then there exists a map
\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{Z}^{n}(F)
for a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
, where
\mathcal{Z}^{n}(F)
is the kernel of the n
{}^{\operatorname{th}}
differential, such that the induced map
\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathcal{F})
is not zero. This gives us a non-zero map from
\Sigma^{-n}\mathcal{F}\to F
, which is a contradiction. So,
\mathcal{H}^{n}(X)=0
for all
n\geq 1
. So, the brutal truncation
\sigma^{\geq 0}F
gives a injective resolution of
\mathcal{Z}^{0}(F)
, the kernel of the 0
{}^{\operatorname{th}}
differential. If
\mathcal{Z}^{0}(F)
is not injective, by Baer’s criterion (see for example
[
Kra22
, Lemma 11.2.10]
),
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{Z}^{0}(F))\neq 0
for some coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
.
Note that a non-zero element of
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{Z}^{0}(F))
corresponds to a non-zero map
\Sigma^{-1}\mathcal{F}\to F
. And so, there exists a non-zero map
\Sigma^{-1}\mathcal{F}\to F
for a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
, which is a contradiction. Therefore,
\mathcal{Z}^{0}(F)
is injective. Now, we have a short exact sequence of complexes as follows,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{-2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}^{0}(F)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{-2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{0}/\mathcal{Z}^{0}(F)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

Note that this gives us a triangle in
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
as all of the columns are split short exact sequences. The columns in degree not equal to 0 split trivially, and the column in degree 0 splits as
\mathcal{Z}^{0}(F)
is injective. Further, this implies that
F^{0}/\mathcal{Z}^{0}(F)
is also injective, as it is a summand of the injective object
F^{0}
. As
\mathcal{H}^{n}(F)=0
for all
n\geq 1
, the complex given by the bottom row is in fact acyclic. As it is a bounded below acyclic complex of injectives, it is in fact further null-homotopic. So, we get that
F
is isomorphic to the complex given by the top row, and hence
F\in\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\leq 0}
, which is what we needed to show.
∎

Theorem 8.6
.

Let
X
be a noetherian scheme. Consider the homotopy category of injectives
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
.
We endow the category of compacts
\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
with the metric which is given by
\{\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\cap\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
, where
(\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq 0},\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\leq 0})
is the standard co-t-structure on
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
defined via brutal truncations. Then,

(1)

\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))\cong\mathbf{D}^{+}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
, see
Theorem

2.16
for the definition of
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))
. Note that this agrees with
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{+}_{c}
if
\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))=\overline{\langle G\rangle}_{N}
for an object
G\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
and
N\geq 1
by
Corollary

8.4
, see
Definition

7.6
, and
Remark

7.13
,
Remark

7.20
.

(2)

\mathcal{Y}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)))=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\leq n}
, see
Definition

8.1
(2).

(3)

\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbf{K}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)\cap\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)=\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))\cong\mathfrak{S}({\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)})
, see
Definition

8.1
(3) and
Theorem

2.16
(3). Further, this agrees with
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{b}_{c}
if
\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))=\overline{\langle G\rangle}_{N}
for an object
G\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
and
N\geq 1
by
Corollary

8.4
, see
Definition

7.6
, and
Remark

7.13
,
Remark

7.20

Proof.

(1)

Let
F\in\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
. Then, by definition, we can choose a sequence
E_{1}\to E_{2}\to E_{3}\to E_{4}\to\cdots
in
\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
mapping to
F
such that
\operatorname{Cone}(E_{n}\to E_{n+i})\in\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq n+1}
for all
n,i\geq 1
, and
\operatornamewithlimits{\underset{\boldsymbol{\xrightarrow{\hskip 24.18501pt}}}{{Hocolim}}}E_{n}\cong F
. And so,
\operatorname{Cone}(E_{n}\to F)\in\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq n}
for all
n\geq 1
. This shows that
F\in\mathbf{K}^{+}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq n}
. Now, we use the equivalence of categories
p:\mathbf{D}^{+}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))\to\mathbf{K}^{+}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
, see
Lemma

7.18
. As we have
\operatorname{Cone}(E_{n}\to F)\in\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq n}=\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))^{\geq n}
for all
n\geq 1
, we get that
H^{i}(E_{n})\cong H^{i}(F)
for all
i<n
. So,
F\in\mathbf{D}^{+}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
.

Conversely, for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{+}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
, the canonical truncations give us give us a Cauchy sequence in
\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
,

\tau^{\leq 0}F\to\tau^{\leq 1}F\to\tau^{\leq 2}F\to\tau^{\leq 3}F\to\cdots

Note that
F\cong\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits\tau^{\leq n}F
as complexes. So, we have that by
[
BN93
, Remark 2.2]
,
F\cong\operatornamewithlimits{\underset{\boldsymbol{\xrightarrow{\hskip 24.18501pt}}}{{Hocolim}}}\tau^{\leq n}F
in the derived category.

(2)

Note that by
Theorem

2.16
(2),

\mathcal{Y}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)))=\bigcup_{n\geq 1}\left(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\cap\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq n}\right)^{\perp}

But, note that
\left(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\cap\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq n}\right)^{\perp}=\left(\bigcup_{i\geq n}\Sigma^{-i}\operatorname{Coh}(X)\right)^{\perp}
, which is the same as
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\leq n-1}
by
Lemma

8.5
.

(3)

Note that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))\cap\mathcal{Y}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)))\cong\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))\cong\mathfrak{S}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))
, see
Theorem

2.16
(3). So, (3) follows from parts (1) and (2).

∎

We note that the metric used in
Theorem

8.6
can be intrinsically defined up to equivalence.

Theorem 8.7
.

Let
X
be a noetherian scheme, then the metric
\{\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\cap\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq n}\}
is equivalent to the intrinsically defined metric
\{\Sigma^{-n}\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))\}
, where
\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))
is a full subcategory of
\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
such that
\{\Sigma^{-n}\mathcal{Q}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
is the finest metric (up to equivalence) in the following collection,

\left\{\{\Sigma^{-n}\mathcal{P}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}:\mathcal{P}=G(-\infty,0]^{\perp}\text{ for some }G\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\right\}

see
Definition

3.3
for the order relation on metrics.

Proof.

Observe that the metric
\{\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\cap\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{\geq n}\}
is the same as the metric
\{\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\cap\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(X)^{\geq n}\}
. Then, this follows by
[
Nee18a
, Theorem 6.3]
, as
\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)=\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}
for the noetherian triangulated category
\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(X)
, see
Theorem

2.24
.
∎

From this, we get the following corollary

Corollary 8.8
.

For a noetherian scheme
X
, there is an intrinsic way to construct
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
from
\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
.

Under the assumption of the existence of a dualizing complex,
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
is equivalent to the category of perfect complexes.

Theorem 8.9
.

Let
X
be a noetherian scheme with a dualizing complex
\omega\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
. Then, we have an equivalence
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)\cong\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(X)

Proof.

The map
-\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\omega:\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(R)\to\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
gives us the required equivalence. We can check this locally. But the local result is immediate from
[
IK06
, Proposition 4.7]
using the fact that the functors in the statement preserve coherent cohomology.
∎

We have analogous result for the mock homotopy category of projectives.

Theorem 8.10
.

Let
X
be a noetherian, separated scheme. Consider the mock homotopy category of projectives
\mathbf{K}_{m}(\operatorname{Proj}\text{-}X)
.
We endow the category of compacts
\mathbf{K}_{m}(\operatorname{Proj}\text{-}X)^{c}\cong\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)^{\operatorname{op}}
(see
[
Mur08
, Theorem 7.4]
) with the metric which is given by
\{\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)^{\leq-n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
. Then, with notation as in
[
Mur08
]
,

(1)

\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))=U_{\lambda}(D^{-}(\operatorname{coh}(X))^{\circ}
, see
Theorem

2.16
for the definition of
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))
. Note that this agrees with
\mathbf{K}_{m}(\operatorname{Proj}\text{-}X)^{+}_{c}
if
\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))=\overline{\langle G\rangle}_{N}
for an object
G\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
and
N\geq 1
by
Corollary

8.4
, see
Definition

7.6
, and
Remark

7.13
,
Remark

7.25
.

(2)

\mathcal{Y}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)))=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{n}\mathsf{V}
where
(\mathsf{U},\mathsf{V})
is the co-t-structure of
Convention

7.22
.

(3)

\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(X)=\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X))\cong\mathfrak{S}({\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)})
, see
Definition

8.1
(3) and
Theorem

2.16
(3). Note that this agrees with
\mathbf{K}_{m}(\operatorname{Proj}\text{-}X)^{b}_{c}
if we have that
\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))=\overline{\langle G\rangle}_{N}
for an object
G\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
and
N\geq 1
by
Corollary

8.4
, see
Definition

7.6
,
Remark

7.13
, and
Remark

7.25
.

Proof.

(1)

The proof is analogous to
Theorem

8.6
(1) by using
[
Mur08
, Lemma 7.3]

(2)

Obvious from definition.

(3)

Follows by
[
Nee18a
, Proposition 5.6]
using
[
Mur08
, Lemma 7.3]
.

∎

We have the following result for the derived category of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Theorem 8.11
.

For all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
G_{i}
be the quasicoherent sheaf corresponding to the 1-dimensional weight
i
representation. Let
\mathsf{T}=\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
. We equip
\mathsf{T}
with the generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
of
Remark

7.29
, and
\mathsf{T}^{c}
with the metric
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{G}}
, see
Definition

3.8
(2). Then,

(1)

\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}=\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})=\left\{\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{i}G_{j}^{\oplus n_{ij}}:n_{ij}\in\mathbb{N}\text{ for all i,j}\right\}

(2)

\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}=\mathfrak{S}({\mathsf{T}^{c}})\cong\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})=\mathsf{T}^{c}

Proof.

The computation of
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}{\mathsf{T}^{c}})
and
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})
is straightforward from the definitions, and the structure of the derived category, see
Lemma

7.28
and
Remark

7.29
. Further, as
\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
is
\mathcal{G}
-approximable, see
Theorem

7.30
, we have that
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}=\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})
and
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}=\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T}^{c})
by
Corollary

8.4
.
∎

8.1.
Applications of the representability theorems

We now state some applications of the abstract representability results of the previous chapter, using the computations of the closure of the compacts done above. We don’t mention the examples involving approximable triangulated categories here, as those are already covered in
[
Nee21b
, Theorem 0.3]
.

Theorem 8.12
.

Let
X
be a proper scheme over a noetherian ring
R
such that
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
is co-approximable, see
Theorem

7.26
for examples. Let
\mathcal{Y}:\mathbf{D}^{+}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)^{\operatorname{op}},\operatorname{Mod}(R))
be the restricted Yoneda functor. Then,

(i)

\mathcal{Y}
is full, and the essential image is the cohomological functors
H
such that
H(\Sigma^{i}F)=0
for
i>>0
for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
.

(ii)

\mathcal{Y}
gives an equivalence of categories between
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
and the finite cohomological functors, that is, cohomological functors
H
such that
H(\Sigma^{i}F)=0
for
|i|>>0
for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
. Recall that
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)=\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\cap\mathbf{K}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
.

Proof.

Recall that a co-approximable triangulated category is an example of a
\mathcal{G}
-approximable triangulated category, see
Theorem

7.10
. We have computed the closure of the compacts
\mathsf{T}^{+}_{c}
(see
Remark

7.13
) and the bounded objects in the closure of the compacts
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}
for
\mathsf{T}=\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)
in
Theorem

8.6
. We have that,

\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{+}_{c}\cong\mathbf{D}^{+}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)\text{ and }\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)^{b}_{c}\cong\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}X)

see
Theorem

8.6
(1) and (3).

Then (i) and (ii) follow directly from
Theorem

6.22
and
Theorem

6.24
respectively. Note that the properness of the scheme ensures that
\operatorname{Hom}(D,F)
is a finitely generated
R
-module for any
D,F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
. Further, this description of the
\mathcal{G}
-semifinite and
\mathcal{G}
-finite
\mathsf{T}^{c}
-cohomological functors comes from
Example

6.7
(2).
∎

Remark 8.13
.

If in
Theorem

8.12
,
X
has a dualizing complex, then there is an equivalence between the category of finite cohomological functors on
\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
and the category
\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(X)
by
Theorem

8.9
.

We have results analogous to
Theorem

8.12
for coherent algebras and stacks with the same proofs using
Remark

7.21
, which we write down in the remarks below.

Remark 8.14
.

Let
X
be a proper scheme over a noetherian ring
R
, and
\mathcal{A}
an Azumaya algebra such that
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}\mathcal{A})
is co-approximable, see
Theorem

7.26
for examples. Let
\mathcal{Y}:\mathbf{D}^{+}_{\operatorname{coh}}(\mathcal{A})\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(\mathcal{A})^{\operatorname{op}},\operatorname{Mod}(R))
be the restricted Yoneda functor. Then,

(i)

\mathcal{Y}
is full, and the essential image is the cohomological functors
H
such that
H(\Sigma^{i}F)=0
for
i>>0
for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(\mathcal{A})
.

(ii)

\mathcal{Y}
gives an equivalence of categories between
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}\mathcal{A})
and the finite cohomological functors, that is, cohomological functors
H
such that
H(\Sigma^{i}F)=0
for
|i|>>0
for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(\mathcal{A})
. Recall that
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}\mathcal{A})=\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(\mathcal{A})\cap\mathbf{K}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}\mathcal{A})
.

Remark 8.15
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a proper stack over a noetherian ring
R
such that
\mathbf{K}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}\mathcal{X})
is co-approximable, see
Theorem

7.27
for examples. Let
\mathcal{Y}:\mathbf{D}^{+}_{\operatorname{coh}}(\mathcal{X})\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(\mathcal{X})^{\operatorname{op}},\operatorname{Mod}(R))
be the restricted Yoneda functor. Then,

(i)

\mathcal{Y}
is full, and the essential image is the cohomological functors
H
such that
H(\Sigma^{i}F)=0
for
i>>0
for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(\mathcal{X})
.

(ii)

\mathcal{Y}
gives an equivalence of categories between
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}\mathcal{X})
and the finite cohomological functors, that is, cohomological functors
H
such that
H(\Sigma^{i}F)=0
for
|i|>>0
for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(\mathcal{X})
. Recall that
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}\mathcal{X})=\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(\mathcal{X})\cap\mathbf{K}^{b}(\operatorname{Inj}\text{-}\mathcal{X})
.

Now we state a representability theorem which follows from the co-quasiapproximability of the mock homotopy category of projectives.

Theorem 8.16
.

Let
X
be a proper scheme over a noetherian ring
R
such that
\mathbf{K}_{m}(\operatorname{Proj}\text{-}X)
is co-quasiapproximable, see
Theorem

7.26
for examples. We have the restricted Yoneda functor
\mathcal{Y}:\mathbf{D}^{-}(\operatorname{coh}\text{-}X)^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X),\operatorname{Mod}(R))
. Then,

(i)

\mathcal{Y}
is full, and the essential image is the homological functors
H
such that
H(\Sigma^{i}F)=0
for
i>>0
for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
.

(ii)

\mathcal{Y}
gives an equivalence of categories between
\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(X)
and the finite homological functors, that is, homological functors
H
such that
H(\Sigma^{i}F)=0
for
|i|>>0
for any
F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
.

Proof.

Recall that a co-quasiapproximable triangulated category is an example of a
\mathcal{G}
-quasiapproximable triangulated category, see
Theorem

7.10
. We have computed the closure of the compacts
\mathsf{T}^{+}_{c}
(see
Remark

7.13
) and the bounded objects in the closure of the compacts
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}
for
\mathsf{T}=\mathbf{K}_{m}(\operatorname{Proj}\text{-}X)
in
Theorem

8.10
. We have that,

\mathbf{K}_{m}(\operatorname{Proj}\text{-}X)^{+}_{c}\cong\mathbf{D}^{-}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)^{\operatorname{op}}\text{ and }\mathbf{K}_{m}(\operatorname{Proj}\text{-}X)^{b}_{c}\cong\mathbf{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(X)

see
Theorem

8.10
(1) and (3).

Then, (i) and (ii) follow directly from
Theorem

6.22
and
Theorem

6.24
respectively. Note that the properness of the scheme ensures that
\operatorname{Hom}(D,F)
is a finitely generated
R
-module for any
D,F\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
.
∎

Remark 8.17
.

Note that at the moment, the examples of co-approximable triangulated categories all come via strong generation. The reader may wonder on the relation between
Theorem

8.12
and the representability results in the literature involving strong generation.

(1)

One of the earliest and most famous of this form of results is
[
BvdB03
, Theorem 1.3]
. But, this does not apply here as this category is not Ext-finite in general.

(2)

For a noetherian, separated scheme
X
such that
\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))=\overline{\langle G\rangle}_{N}
for some object
G\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
and a positive integer
N
, the conclusion of
Theorem

8.12
(ii) follows from
[
Nee18b
, Theorem 0.3]
.

(3)

For a noetherian, separated scheme
X
with a dualizing complex satisfying the condition
\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Qcoh}(X))=\overline{\langle G\rangle}_{N}
for some object
G\in\mathbf{D}^{b}_{\operatorname{coh}}(X)
and a positive integer
N
, the conclusion of
Theorem

8.12
(ii) follows from
[
Nee18b
, Theorem 0.3]
.

We now apply the representability theorems to the derived category of the classifying stack of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Note that due to the simple nature of the derived category in this particular case, these result can be shown quite trivially using other methods. The main point of this result is to show we can apply the representability theorems to triangulated categories which do not have a single compact generator. That being said, it is important to note that the definition of
\mathcal{G}
-semifinite and
\mathcal{G}
-finite in this case depends on the generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
. Compare this with
Example

6.7
, where these have an intrinsic description.

Theorem 8.18
.

Let
\mathsf{T}=\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
and denote the restricted Yoneda functor
\mathcal{Y}:\mathsf{T}\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathsf{T}^{c},\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbb{C}))
. We endow
\mathsf{T}
with the generating sequence
\mathcal{G}
of
Remark

7.29
. Then,

(1)

\mathcal{Y}
is full on the subcategory
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
, where,

\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}=\left\{\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Sigma^{i}G_{j}^{\oplus n_{ij}}:n_{ij}\in\mathbb{N}\text{ for all i,j}\right\}

from
Theorem

8.11
(1),
and the essential image is the
\mathcal{G}
-semifinite cohomological functors. In this case, these are exactly the cohomological functors
H:[\mathsf{T}^{c}]^{\operatorname{op}}\to\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbb{C})
such that for any
F\in\mathsf{T}^{c}
,
H(\Sigma^{i}F)
is a finite vector space for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

\mathcal{Y}
gives an equivalence of categories between
\mathsf{T}^{c}
and the
\mathcal{G}
-finite cohomological functors on
\mathsf{T}^{c}
.

Proof.

We know that
\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
is
\mathcal{G}
-approximable from
Theorem

7.30
. Further, we have computed
\overline{\mathsf{T}^{c}}
and
\mathsf{T}^{b}_{c}
for
\mathsf{T}=\mathbf{D}_{\operatorname{Qcoh}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
in
Theorem

8.11
. So, (1) and (2) follow from from
Theorem

6.22
and
Theorem

6.24
respectively.
∎
